In an inhomogeneous medium\n56\tthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n57\t\n58\tCapture-Safe Stepping\n59\t---------------------\n60\tAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n61\t\n62\t- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n63\t- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n64\t- ``time``: float current simulation time\n65\t\n66\tSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\n67\targuments. No closure captures are used in kernel definitions.\n68\t\n69\tReferences\n70\t---------\n71\t- Vacuum update: ``../meep/src/update_eh.cpp``\n72\t- Kernel formula sources: dHy_dz)\n241\t\n242\t    @wp.kernel\n243\t    def magnetic_update_3d(\n244\t        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n245\t        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n246\t        mu_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n247\t        mu_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n248\t        mu_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n249\t        dt: float,\n250\t        dx: float,\n251\t        dy: float,\n252\t        dz: float,\n253\t        nx: int,\n254\t        ny: int,\n255\t        nz: int,\n256\t    ):\n257\t        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n258\t\n259\t        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the\n260\t        curl of the electric field. Maxwell timestepping kernels for Phase 1.\n\nThis module provides the GPU-accelerated E and H field update kernels.\nThe kernels follow the Warp backend conventions documented in ``backend.py``.\n\nKernel Organization\n------------------\nThe Maxwell update is split into explicit stages:\n\n1. **boundary_exchange** — fill ghost cells before each update\n2. **electric_update** — update electric field components\n3. **source_injection** — add source contributions to E\n4. **boundary_exchange** — re-exchange E ghosts before H update\n5. **magnetic_update** — update magnetic field components\n6. **pml_stage** — apply PML/ABC damping (post-H update)\n7. **convergence_check** — integrated field intensity (Python-side)\n\nUpdate Equations\n---------------\nFor a Yee lattice with cell sizes ``(dx, dy, dz)`` and timestep ``dt``:\n\nElectric field update::\n\n    Ex[i+1] = Ex[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hz[i,j+1,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Hy[i,j,k])/dx - (Hx[i,j+1,k] - Hx[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nMagnetic field update::\n\n    Hx[i+1] = Hx[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ez[i,j+1,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dy - (Ey[i,j,k+1] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nCoefficients\n-----------\nFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n\n    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n\nIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\nthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n\n- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n- ``time``: float current simulation time\n\nSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\narguments. No closure captures are used in kernel definitions.\n\nReferences\n---------\n- Vacuum update: ``../meep/src/update_eh.cpp``\n- Kernel formula sources: ``../phase1/kernel-formula-sources.md``\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom autofdtd.kernels.backend import (\n    WARP_AVAILABLE,\n    ComplexFieldPolicy,\n    WarpTimer,\n    allocate_coefficient_array,\n    allocate_field_array,\n    backend_info,\n    nvtx_range,\n    step_metrics,\n    wp,\n)\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"electric_update_3d\",\n    \"magnetic_update_3d\",\n    \"MaxwellStepKernelSpec\",\n    \"allocate_maxwell_arrays\",\n    \"step_maxwell\",\n    \"step_kernel_metadata\",\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Kernel metadata\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass MaxwellStepKernelSpec:\n    \"\"\"Metadata for a Maxwell timestep kernel.\n\n    Attributes\n    ----------\n    name : str\n        Kernel function name.\n    field_family : str\n        \"electric\" or \"magnetic\".\n    uses_pml : bool\n        Whether this kernel requires PML coefficient arrays.\n    uses_sources : bool\n        Whether this kernel has source injection integrated.\n    supports_complex : bool\n        Whether this kernel supports complex-valued fields.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    field_family: Literal[\"electric\", \"magnetic\"]\n    uses_pml: bool = False\n    uses_sources: bool = False\n    supports_complex: bool = True\n\n\ndef step_kernel_metadata() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Expose the current stepping kernel metadata for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n    return {\n        \"backend\": \"warp\" if WARP_AVAILABLE else \"numpy\",\n        \"warp_available\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"module_contents_stable\": False,  # Set True after kernels are finalized\n        \"electric_update\": MaxwellStepKernelSpec(\n            name=\"electric_update_3d\",\n            field_family=\"electric\",\n            uses_pml=False,\n            uses_sources=False,\n            supports_complex=True,\n        ),\n        \"magnetic_update\": MaxwellStepKernelSpec(\n            name=\"magnetic_update_3d\",\n            field_family=\"magnetic\",\n            uses_pml=False,\n            uses_sources=False,\n            supports_complex=True,\n        ),\n        \"update_stages\": (\n            \"boundary_exchange\",\n            \"electric_update\",\n            \"source_injection\",\n            \"boundary_exchange\",\n            \"magnetic_update\",\n            \"pml_stage\",\n            \"monitor_collection\",\n            \"convergence_check\",\n        ),\n        \"curl_component_mapping\": {\n            \"Ex\": (\"Hz\", \"dy\", \"Hy\", \"dz\"),  # (curl_component, cofactor1, neighbor_field, cofactor2)\n            \"Ey\": (\"Hz\", \"dz\", \"Hx\", \"dx\"),\n            \"Ez\": (\"Hy\", \"dx\", \"Hx\", \"dy\"),\n            \"Hx\": (\"Ez\", \"dy\", \"Ey\", \"dz\"),\n            \"Hy\": (\"Ez\", \"dz\", \"Ex\", \"dx\"),\n            \"Hz\": (\"Ey\", \"dx\", \"Ex\", \"dy\"),\n        },\n        \"coefficient_requires\": (\"eps_xx\", \"eps_yy\", \"eps_zz\", \"mu_xx\", \"mu_yy\", \"mu_zz\"),\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Warp kernel definitions\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# These are the core Maxwell update kernels. They are defined using Warp's\n# @wp.kernel decorator for JIT compilation. The actual implementations follow\n# the Yee lattice update equations documented in this module's docstring.\n\nif WARP_AVAILABLE:\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        eps_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        eps_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        eps_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using the\n        curl of the magnetic field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n\n        Args:\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n            eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz: Permittivity arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n            dt: Timestep size\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - skip ghost cells\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        idx = i + j * nx + k * nx * ny\n\n        # Ex[i,j,k] update using curl(H) at (i, j+1/2, k+1/2)\n        # dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n        Hz_im1 = H[(i + 1) + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 2 * nx * ny * nz]\n        Hz_i = H[i + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 2 * nx * ny * nz]\n        Hy_im1 = H[(i + 1) + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 1 * nx * ny * nz]\n        Hy_i = H[i + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 1 * nx * ny * nz]\n\n        # Actually we need neighboring cells for the curl\n        # Using simple central difference approximation\n        # Ex update: (Hz[j+1] - Hz[j])/dy - (Hy[k+1] - Hy[k])/dz\n        # For simplicity, use first-order neighbor\n        dHy_dz = (Hy_i - Hy_im1) / dz if k > 0 else 0.0\n        dHz_dy = (Hz_i - Hz_im1) / dy if j > 0 else 0.0\n\n        eps = eps_xx[idx]\n        ce = dt / (eps + 1e-20)  # avoid division by zero\n\n        E[idx] += ce * (dHz_dy - dHy_dz)\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def magnetic_update_3d(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        mu_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        mu_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        mu_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the\n        curl of the electric field. Ghost cells must be filled by\n        boundary exchange before calling this kernel.\n\n        Args:\n            H: Magnetic field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Hx, Hy, Hz)\n            E: Electric field array with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3), component order (Ex, Ey, Ez)\n            mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz: Permeability arrays with shape (nx, ny, nz)\n            dt: Timestep size\n            dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - skip ghost cells\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        idx = i + j * nx + k * nx * ny\n\n        # Similar curl update for magnetic field\n        # Using the same simple approximation as electric update\n        mu = mu_xx[idx]\n        ch = -dt / (mu + 1e-20)  # note the negative sign for H update\n\n        H[idx] += ch * 0.0  # Placeholder - full curl computation needed\n\nelse:\n    # Stub definitions when Warp is unavailable\n    electric_update_3d = None\n    magnetic_update_3d = None\n\n\n# {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_9jvnvquo8xwk_2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Warp backend conventions for Phase 1 GPU acceleration.\n2\t\n3\tThis module establishes the foundational backend contracts that all kernel modules\n4\tmust follow. It provides:\n5\t\n6\t- Device management and GPU selection policies\n7\t- Persistent array base types and allocation conventions\n8\t- Module stability markers for JIT compilation\n9\t- Capture-safe stepping protocol\n10\t- Profiling hooks and instrumentation\n11\t\n12\tDesign Principles\n13\t----------------\n14\t1. **Module stability before JIT churn**: All kernels in a module compile together.\n15\t   Stabilize module contents early to avoid reload churn.\n16\t2. **Persistent arrays over temporaries**: Field buffers are allocated once and\n17\t   reused across timesteps to minimize allocation overhead.\n18\t3. **Capture-safe stepping**: Kernels must not capture mutable state from the\n19\t   outer Python scope. All timestep-dependent values must be passed as arguments.\n20\t4. **Explicit device placement**: Arrays carry device affinity explicitly rather\n21\t   than relying on global state.\n22\t5. **Structured profiling**: Use Warp's ScopedTimer and NVTX markers for\n23\t   GPU-side instrumentation, with CPU-side timing hooks for end-to-end metrics.\n24\t\n25\tDevice Management\n26\t-----------------\n27\t- Use ``get_warp_device()`` to obtain the default device or a specific GPU.\n28\t- For multi-GPU: map logical chunk indices to device IDs via\n29\t  ``ChunkLayout.device_assignment``.\n30\t- All array allocations use the target device explicitly.\n31\t\n32\tArray Conventions\n33\t----------------\n34\t- Field arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32 or wp.complex64, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3))``\n35\t- Coefficient arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n36\t- Auxiliary state: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.complex64, shape=(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n37\t- All arrays are allocated on the target device; no managed memory unless explicit.\n38\t\n39\tDtype Policy\n40\t-----------\n41\t- Single precision (float32/complex64) is the default for GPU execution.\n42\t- Double precision (float64/complex128) is available but may significantly\n43\t  reduce performance on consumer NVIDIA GPUs.\n44\t- Complex fields are required when any Bloch boundary is present.\n45\t- The ``requires_complex_fields`` flag from ``BoundarySpecIR`` determines whether\n46\t  to use complex dtype for field storage.\n47\t\n48\tModule Stability\n49\t---------------\n50\tUse ``mark_module_stable()`` after defining all kernels in a module.\n51\tThis signals that the module's kernel set is frozen and ready for JIT.\n52\tDuring development, unstable modules trigger a warning on import.\n53\t\n54\tCapture-Safe Stepping\n55\t---------------------\n56\tEvery kernel function that depends on timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index)\n57\tmust receive those values as explicit arguments. Do NOT capture them from closure.\n58\t\n59\tExample::\n60\t\n61\t    # BAD - captures dt from outer scope\n62\t    @wp.kernel\n63\t    def bad_update(E, H,):\n64\t        dt = outer_dt  # captured!\n65\t        ...\n66\t\n67\t    # GOOD - dt passed explicitly\n68\t    @wp.kernel\n69\t    def good_update(E, H, dt: float):\n70\t        ...\n71\t\n72\tCapture-safe stepping also means:\n73\t- Source amplitudes are computed outside the kernel and passed as arrays or scalars\n74\t- Monitor intervals are evaluated in Python before calling recording kernels\n75\t- Boundary coefficients are pre-compiled and passed as array arguments\n76\t\n77\tProfiling Hooks\n78\t---------------\n79\t- ``WarpTimer`` context manager for GPU-side kernel timing\n80\t- ``nvtx_range`` for annotating execution phases\n81\t- ``step_metrics()`` helper for computing cells/s and Gcells/s\n82\t\n83\tReferences\n84\t----------\n85\t- Warp docs: https://docs.nvidia.com/warp-py\n86\t- GPU Benchmarking: ``../papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf``\n87\t- Chunk Contract: ``../phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md``\n88\t\"\"\"\n89\t\n90\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n91\t\n92\tfrom contextlib import contextmanager\n93\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n94\tfrom typing import Any, Literal\n95\t\n96\t__all__ = [\n97\t    \"WARP_AVAILABLE\",\n98\t    \"wp\",\n99\t    \"get_warp_device\",\n100\t    \"get_default_device\",\n101\t    \"allocate_field_array\",\n102\t    \"allocate_coefficient_array\",\n103\t    \"allocate_auxiliary_array\",\n104\t    \"FieldArrayTag\",\n105\t    \"CoefficientArrayTag\",\n106\t    \"AuxiliaryArrayTag\",\n107\t    \"ArrayTags\",\n108\t    \"WarpTimer\",\n109\t    \"nvtx_range\",\n110\t    \"step_metrics\",\n111\t    \"backend_info\",\n112\t    \"WarpBackendInfo\",\n113\t    \"ComplexFieldPolicy\",\n114\t    \"ModuleStability\",\n115\t    \"mark_module_stable\",\n116\t    \"is_module_stable\",\n117\t]\n118\t\n119\t\n120\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n121\t# Warp import with graceful fallback\n122\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n123\t\n124\ttry:\n125\t    import warp as wp\n126\texcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # backend conventions for Phase 1 GPU acceleration.\n\nThis module establishes the foundational backend contracts that all kernel modules\nmust follow. It provides:\n\n- Device management and GPU selection policies\n- Persistent array base types and allocation conventions\n- Module stability markers for JIT compilation\n- Capture-safe stepping protocol\n- Profiling hooks and instrumentation\n\nDesign Principles\n----------------\n1. **Module stability before JIT churn**: All kernels in a module compile together.\n   Stabilize module contents early to avoid reload churn.\n2. **Persistent arrays over temporaries**: Field buffers are allocated once and\n   reused across timesteps to minimize allocation overhead.\n3. **Capture-safe stepping**: Kernels must not capture mutable state from the\n   outer Python scope. All timestep-dependent values must be passed as arguments.\n4. **Explicit device placement**: Arrays carry device affinity explicitly rather\n   than relying on global state.\n5. **Structured profiling**: Use Warp's ScopedTimer and NVTX markers for\n   GPU-side instrumentation, with CPU-side timing hooks for end-to-end metrics.\n\nDevice Management\n-----------------\n- Use ``get_warp_device()`` to obtain the default device or a specific GPU.\n- For multi-GPU: map logical chunk indices to device IDs via\n  ``ChunkLayout.device_assignment``.\n- All array allocations use the target device explicitly.\n\nArray Conventions\n----------------\n- Field arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32 or wp.complex64, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3))``\n- Coefficient arrays: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.float32, shape=(Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n- Auxiliary state: ``wp.array(dtype=wp.complex64, shape=(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz))``\n- All arrays are allocated on the target device; no managed memory unless explicit.\n\nDtype Policy\n-----------\n- Single precision (float32/complex64) is the default for GPU execution.\n- Double precision (float64/complex128) is available but may significantly\n  reduce performance on consumer NVIDIA GPUs.\n- Complex fields are required when any Bloch boundary is present.\n- The ``requires_complex_fields`` flag from ``BoundarySpecIR`` determines whether\n  to use complex dtype for field storage.\n\nModule Stability\n---------------\nUse ``mark_module_stable()`` after defining all kernels in a module.\nThis signals that the module's kernel set is frozen and ready for JIT.\nDuring development, unstable modules trigger a warning on import.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nEvery kernel function that depends on timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index)\nmust receive those values as explicit arguments. Do NOT capture them from closure.\n\nExample::\n\n    # BAD - captures dt from outer scope\n    @wp.kernel\n    def bad_update(E, H,):\n        dt = outer_dt  # captured!\n        ...\n\n    # GOOD - dt passed explicitly\n    @wp.kernel\n    def good_update(E, H, dt: float):\n        ...\n\nCapture-safe stepping also means:\n- Source amplitudes are computed outside the kernel and passed as arrays or scalars\n- Monitor intervals are evaluated in Python before calling recording kernels\n- Boundary coefficients are pre-compiled and passed as array arguments\n\nProfiling Hooks\n---------------\n- ``WarpTimer`` context manager for GPU-side kernel timing\n- ``nvtx_range`` for annotating execution phases\n- ``step_metrics()`` helper for computing cells/s and Gcells/s\n\nReferences\n----------\n- Warp docs: https://docs.nvidia.com/warp-py\n- GPU Benchmarking: ``../papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf``\n- Chunk Contract: ``../phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md``\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom contextlib import contextmanager\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom typing import Any, Literal\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"WARP_AVAILABLE\",\n    \"wp\",\n    \"get_warp_device\",\n    \"get_default_device\",\n    \"allocate_field_array\",\n    \"allocate_coefficient_array\",\n    \"allocate_auxiliary_array\",\n    \"FieldArrayTag\",\n    \"CoefficientArrayTag\",\n    \"AuxiliaryArrayTag\",\n    \"ArrayTags\",\n    \"WarpTimer\",\n    \"nvtx_range\",\n    \"step_metrics\",\n    \"backend_info\",\n    \"WarpBackendInfo\",\n    \"ComplexFieldPolicy\",\n    \"ModuleStability\",\n    \"mark_module_stable\",\n    \"is_module_stable\",\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Warp import with graceful fallback\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ntry:\n    import warp as wp\nexcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # pragma: no cover - Check for Bloch axes in boundary spec\n            if hasattr(boundary_spec, \"bloch_axes\"):\n                has_bloch = len(getattr(boundary_spec, \"bloch_axes\", ())) > 0\n            elif hasattr(boundary_spec, \"axes\"):\n                for axis_bdry in boundary_spec.axes():\n                    for side in (\"minus\", \"plus\"):\n                        edge = getattr(axis_bdry, side, None)\n                        if edge and hasattr(edge, \"mode\"):\n                            if str(edge.mode).lower() == \"bloch\":\n                                has_bloch = True\n                                break\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n\n        return cls(has_bloch_axis=has_bloch)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Backend exports for kernel metadata\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef backend_summary() -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Return a summary dict of backend state for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n    info = backend_info()\n    return {\n        \"warp_available\": info.warp_available,\n        \"warp_version\": info.warp_version,\n        \"cuda_available\": info.cuda_available,\n        \"num_devices\": info.num_devices,\n        \"current_device\": info.current_device,\n        \"supports_graph_capture\": info.supports_graph_capture,\n        \"supports_float64\": info.supports_float64,\n        \"stable_modules\": list(_module_stability.stable_modules),\n    }\n","numLines":692,"startLine":1,"totalLines":692}}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_9jvnvquo8xwk_3","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Phase 1 Chunk Contract and Multi-Node Decomposition Architecture\n2\t\n3\t## Status\n4\t\n5\t**Draft — for review and task handoff**\n6\t\n7\t## 1. Background and Design Motivation\n8\t\n9\tPhase 1 (`task-047`) establishes the architectural contract for chunk-based decomposition before solver kernels are wired to a runtime executor. The goal is to define what a chunk owns, how chunks expose halo and monitor metadata, how chunk placement maps onto devices or nodes, and how this avoids blocking later systolic or overlapped execution phases.\n10\t\n11\t### Reference Foundations\n12\t\n13\t- **Meep chunk architecture** (`../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`): chunk is the stable decomposition unit, not MPI rank. A rank may own multiple chunks. Chunk boundaries align with PML layers and symmetry planes, not with MPI ranks. Communication plans are stage-specific and component-family-specific.\n14\t- **Systolic FDTD scheduling** (`papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`): the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy. Per-face, per-stage halo semantics and interior-versus-boundary planning must be explicit now so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n15\t- **Phase 1 codebase state**: `CompiledSimulation` in `compiler/pipeline.py` treats the entire grid as one monolithic entity. `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` is a global constant. No chunk index, rank, ownership mask, or per-chunk field decomposition exists yet.\n16\t\n17\t### Design Principles\n18\t\n19\t1. **Chunk is the unit of parallelism.** Rank or device is placement, not computation.\n20\t2. **Logical and stored topology diverge.** Symmetry reduction and periodic/Bloch remapping are logical-address transforms over a full-domain view, not data-layout changes.\n21\t3. **Scene compilation is separate from stepping.** Geometry and material initialization produce coefficient fields; those fields are then chunked for execution.\n22\t4. **Bulk-synchronous by default, systolic-ready by design.** Phase 1 is bulk-synchronous, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics so overlap can be layered in later without rearchitecting the chunk contract.\n23\t5. **Halo depth is per-face, not global.** Different boundary families (PML, ABC, Bloch, periodic) require different halo widths and different exchange semantics.\n24\t\n25\t---\n26\t\n27\t## 2. Chunk Data Model\n28\t\n29\t### 2.1 ChunkSpec — Planning Metadata\n30\t\n31\t`ChunkSpec` is a frozen planning metadata object produced during scene compilation (not a runtime data structure). It describes the intended decomposition before any field buffers are allocated.\n32\t\n33\t```python\n34\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n35\tclass ChunkSpec:\n36\t    \"\"\"Planning metadata for one chunk — produced during compilation.\"\"\"\n37\t\n38\t    chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int]          # logical position in chunk grid\n39\t    global_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]            # full-domain bounds of this chunk (lo, hi)\n40\t    interior_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]          # interior (non-halo) cell index range\n41\t    owned_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]              # cells this chunk updates (including boundary)\n42\t    local_grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int]       # full local array shape incl. ghost cells\n43\t\n44\t    # Per-face halo metadata\n45\t    face_halos: dict[str, FaceHalo]              # key: \"x.minus\", \"x.plus\", \"y.minus\", ...\n46\t\n47\t    # Symmetry metadata\n48\t    is_reduced: bool                             # True if chunk is in symmetry-reduced region\n49\t    symmetry_multiplicity: int                   # how many full-domain points this chunk represents\n50\t\n51\t    # Ownership flags for sources and monitors\n52\t    source_indices: tuple[int, ...]             # indices into compiled_sources owned here\n53\t    monitor_indices: tuple[int, ...]              # indices into compiled_monitors owned here\n54\t```\n55\t\n56\t**Key invariants:**\n57\t- `interior_bounds` defines the region this chunk updates without reading halo data.\n58\t- `owned_bounds` includes the one-cell-wide boundary ring that this chunk writes and other chunks read.\n59\t- `local_grid_shape` is the shape of the full local array including ghost cells — this is what field buffers are allocated to.\n60\t- For a chunk at chunk_index `(i, j, k)`, the global cell index range of its interior is `[interior_bounds.lo, interior_bounds.hi)` in grid coordinates.\n61\t\n62\t### 2.2 FaceHalo — Per-Face Halo Descriptor\n63\t\n64\t```python\n65\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n66\tclass FaceHalo:\n67\t    \"\"\"Halo metadata for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n68\t\n69\t    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n70\t    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n71\t    depth: int                                  # number of ghost cells on this face\n72\t    neighbor_chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int] | None  # None = domain boundary\n73\t    exchange_kind: Literal[\n74\t        \"periodic\", \"bloch\", \"pec\", \"pmc\",\n75\t        \"pml\", \"stable_pml\", \"absorber\", \"abc\",\n76\t        \"symmetry_copy\", \"interior\"\n77\t    ]\n78\t    phase_factor: complex = 1.0 + 0.0j          # for Bloch: e^{i k · d}\n79\t    electric_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection\n80\t    magnetic_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection\n81\t    pml_profile: PMLFaceProfile | None = None   # for PML faces: coefficient profiles\n82\t    abc_coefficients: ABCFaceCoefficients | None = None  # for ABC faces\n83\t```\n84\t\n85\t**Exchange kinds:**\n86\t- `periodic`: copy from neighbor's `plus` face to my `minus` ghost (or vice versa)\n87\t- `bloch`: same as periodic but multiply by `phase_factor` (complex conjugate on minus side for H, no conjugate on E per Maxwell's convention)\n88\t- `pec`: set ghost E to `-E_interior` reflected; H ghost from `+H_interior`\n89\t- `pmc`: opposite parity of PEC\n90\t- `pml` / `stable_pml` / `absorber`: staged damping update applied in PML stage (see Section 5)\n91\t- `abc`: first-order Engquist-Majda absorption via one-step recurrence\n92\t- `symmetry_copy`: fill ghost from interior using electric/magnetic sign transforms\n93\t- `interior`: no exchange — this face is adjacent to another local chunk (same process/device)\n94\t\n95\t### 2.3 ChunkLayout — Global Decomposition Plan\n96\t\n97\t```python\n98\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n99\tclass ChunkLayout:\n100\t    \"\"\"Global chunk decomposition plan — immutable after compilation.\"\"\"\n101\t\n102\t    num_chunks: tuple[int, int, int]           # chunks per axis\n103\t    total_chunks: int                          # product of above\n104\t    chunks: tuple[ChunkSpec, ...]               # one per chunk\n105\t    periodic_axes: tuple[str, ...]             # axes with periodic/Bloch boundary\n106\t    bloch_axes: tuple[str, ...]                 # axes with Bloch boundary\n107\t    exchange_plan: dict[str, ExchangeDescriptor]  # keyed by (\"chunk_a\", \"chunk_b\", \"face\")\n108\t\n109\t    # For device/rank mapping\n110\t    device_assignment: tuple[int, ...]         # chunk_index → device_id (for CUDA)\n111\t    rank_assignment: tuple[int, ...]            # chunk_index → MPI rank\n112\t```\n113\t\n114\t**Key property:** `ChunkLayout.total_chunks` is the number of *logical* chunks, not necessarily the number of MPI ranks or CUDA devices. One rank/device may own multiple chunks.\n115\t\n116\t---\n117\t\n118\t## 3. What a Chunk Owns\n119\t\n120\tA chunk is the unit of **field storage and field update**. Each chunk owns:\n121\t\n122\t### 3.1 Field Arrays\n123\t\n124\tFor a chunk with `local_grid_shape = (Nx, Ny, Nz)`:\n125\t\n126\t| Array | Shape | Staggering |\n127\t|---|---|---|\n128\t| `electric_field` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` | Yee electric cell centers |\n129\t| `magnetic_field` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` | Yee magnetic cell centers |\n130\t| `eps_xx`, `eps_yy`, `eps_zz` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | permittivity on E-grid |\n131\t| `mu_xx`, `mu_yy`, `mu_zz` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | permeability on H-grid |\n132\t| `sigma_e` (optional) | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | electric conductivity |\n133\t| `sigma_h` (optional) | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | magnetic conductivity |\n134\t\n135\t**Note:** These are *local* arrays including ghost cells. The interior region `[interior_bounds.lo, interior_bounds.hi)` contains valid owned field values. Ghost cells are filled by the boundary exchange stage before each update.\n136\t\n137\t### 3.2 Dispersive Auxiliary state\n138\t\n139\tFor PoleResidue media, per pole per component:\n140\t\n141\t```\n142\tpol_x[p, i, j, k], pol_y[p, i, j, k], pol_z[p, i, j, k]  # polarization currents\n143\t```\n144\t\n145\tShape: `(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz)` per component axis. These are allocated only for chunks that contain dispersive material.\n146\t\n147\t### 3.3 Anisotropic Auxiliary State\n148\t\n149\tFor diagonal AnisotropicMedia, the six `eps_xx` etc. arrays above are sufficient (no extra auxiliary state). If Phase 2 introduces fully anisotropic media, additional off-diagonal arrays would be needed.\n150\t\n151\t### 3.4 PML/ABC Boundary state\n152\t\n153\tPer face with PML/ABC:\n154\t\n155\t```\n156\tPML memory: dict[face_key, np.ndarray]   # shape from PMLFaceState.memory_shape\n157\tABC previous: dict[face_key, np.ndarray]  # shape from ABCFaceState.value_shape\n158\t```\n159\t\n160\tThese are allocated per chunk per absorbing face, not global.\n161\t\n162\t### 3.5 Source State\n163\t\n164\tSources are not stored per chunk persistently. Instead, each chunk's `ChunkSpec.source_indices` tells which sources from `CompiledSources` inject into this chunk's domain. At runtime, source injection kernels use the compiled source metadata (placements, amplitude weights) to add source contributions directly into the chunk's `electric_field` or `magnetic_field` arrays.\n165\t\n166\t### 3.6 Monitor State\n167\t\n168\tSimilarly, monitor recording state is per-chunk. For field monitors:\n169\t- `FieldMonitorState` per monitor that overlaps this chunk\n170\t- DFT accumulation buffers for frequency-domain monitors\n171\t\n172\tFor flux monitors:\n173\t- `FluxMonitorState` per monitor with surfaces intersecting this chunk\n174\t\n175\tChunk-local monitor data is gathered by a monitor collection stage after each recording interval.\n176\t\n177\t---\n178\t\n179\t## 4. Halo and Monitor Metadata\n180\t\n181\t### 4.1 Halo Exchange Protocol\n182\t\n183\tHalo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**, following the pattern established in the boundary compiler but scoped per chunk pair:\n184\t\n185\t```\n186\tTimestep stage order per chunk:\n187\t  1. [boundary_exchange]    — fill ghost cells from neighbors\n188\t  2. [electric_update]       — update E in interior + owned-boundary cells\n189\t  3. [source_injection]      — add source contributions to E\n190\t  4. [boundary_exchange]     — re-exchange E ghosts (needed for H update)\n191\t  5. [magnetic_update]        — update H in interior + owned-boundary cells\n192\t  6. [monitor_collection]     — record E/H at monitor points (interval filter)\n193\t  7. [pml_stage]             — apply PML/ABC damping to E and H ghosts\n194\t  8. [convergence_check]     — (on interval) compute integrated E², check shutoff\n195\t```\n196\t\n197\t**Key property:** The `boundary_exchange` stage operates on ghost cells only. Interior cells are never communicated during the timestep — only at the start of the step for halo fill and after the step for PML memory update.\n198\t\n199\t### 4.2 Halo Depth\n200\t\n201\tHalo depth is **per-face**, not a global constant. Typical depths:\n202\t\n203\t| Boundary Type | Halo Depth | Notes |\n204\t|---|---|---|\n205\t| Periodic / Bloch | 1 cell | standard Yee ghost cell |\n206\t| PEC / PMC | 1 cell | ghost set by reflection transform |\n207\t| PML | variable (num_layers) | full PML depth, staggered |\n208\t| ABC (first-order) | 1 cell | single-step recurrence |\n209\t| Symmetry | 1 cell | copy from interior with sign transform |\n210\t| Interior (chunk neighbor) | 1 cell | local memory copy |\n211\t\n212\t**The current `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced** with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` from the `ChunkLayout`.\n213\t\n214\t### 4.3 Monitor Overlap with Chunks\n215\t\n216\tA monitor may span multiple chunks. Monitor metadata in `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` tells which monitors overlap this chunk. Monitor data is accumulated per-chunk and gathered by a global collection pass.\n217\t\n218\tFor a `FieldMonitor` with interval/start semantics:\n219\t- Each chunk computes which of its interior cells fall within the monitor's spatial region\n220\t- Recording is done by the chunk that owns the cell\n221\t- Gathered data is assembled in rank-order for `SimulationData`\n222\t\n223\tFor a `FluxMonitor`:\n224\t- The flux surface may be split across multiple chunks\n225\t- Each chunk computes its portion of the Poynting flux integral\n226\t- Partial sums are reduced across chunks\n227\t\n228\t---\n229\t\n230\t## 5. PML / ABC State Decomposition\n231\t\n232\tPML and ABC state must be decomposed per-chunk per-face. The current monolithic `PMLBoundaryState` in the codebase must be replaced.\n233\t\n234\t### 5.1 PML Face State (per chunk, per face)\n235\t\n236\t```python\n237\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n238\tclass ChunkPMLFaceState:\n239\t    \"\"\"PML state for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n240\t\n241\t    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n242\t    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n243\t    memory_shape: tuple[int, ...]         # transverse shape of the PML layer\n244\t    sigma_profile: np.ndarray               # compiled sigma (sigma_min..sigma_max polynomial)\n245\t    kappa_profile: np.ndarray               # compiled kappa\n246\t    alpha_profile: np.ndarray               # compiled alpha\n247\t    memory: np.ndarray                      # persistent PML memory (attenuation term)\n248\t```\n249\t\n250\t### 5.2 ABC Face State (per chunk, per face)\n251\t\n252\t```python\n253\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n254\tclass ChunkABCFaceState:\n255\t    \"\"\"First-order ABC state for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n256\t\n257\t    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n258\t    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n259\t    value_shape: tuple[int, ...]            # transverse shape of the ABC surface\n260\t    previous_boundary: np.ndarray           # E or H at previous timestep on boundary\n261\t    previous_adjacent: np.ndarray           # E or H at previous timestep one cell inside\n262\t```\n263\t\n264\t---\n265\t\n266\t## 6. Device and Node Placement\n267\t\n268\t### 6.1 CUDA GPU Placement\n269\t\n270\tThe `ChunkLayout.device_assignment` field maps each logical `chunk_index` to a CUDA device ID. For Phase 1 single-GPU or dual-GPU smoke tests:\n271\t\n272\t- All chunks map to device 0 (single-GPU fallback)\n273\t- Or chunks 0..N//2 on device 0, chunks N//2..N on device 1\n274\t\n275\t**This mapping is a planning artifact, not the execution runtime.** The actual field arrays are allocated on the target device via CUDA managed memory or explicit device arrays.\n276\t\n277\t### 6.2 Multi-Node / MPI Placement\n278\t\n279\t`ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` maps each logical chunk to an MPI rank. For Phase 1, this is always all chunks to rank 0 (no MPI decomposition). Later phases extend to per-rank ownership with MPI halo exchange.\n280\t\n281\t**Separation of concerns:**\n282\t- `ChunkSpec` is pure planning metadata — no field data, no communication\n283\t- `ChunkLayout` adds device/rank placement hints\n284\t- Actual field arrays and PML state are allocated at runtime using `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape`\n285\t- Halo exchange uses `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` to know which chunk pairs need to communicate\n286\t\n287\t### 6.3 Warp Kernel Conventions\n288\t\n289\tEach chunk's field arrays are Warp-compatible `wp.array` objects allocated on the target device. Warp kernel launches use chunk-local index ranges:\n290\t\n291\t```python\n292\t# Pseudo-code for chunk-local kernel launch\n293\tchunk = chunks[chunk_id]\n294\twp.launch(\n295\t    electric_update_kernel,\n296\t    dim=chunk.local_grid_shape,\n297\t    inputs=[chunk.electric_field, chunk.magnetic_field, chunk.eps, chunk.mu],\n298\t    device=chunk.device_id,\n299\t)\n300\t```\n301\t\n302\tHalo exchange is implemented as separate Warp kernels that read from neighbor chunks' field arrays and write to ghost cells.\n303\t\n304\t---\n305\t\n306\t## 7. Symmetry and Logical vs. Stored Topology\n307\t\n308\t### 7.1 Symmetry Reduction\n309\t\n310\tWhen `Simulation.symmetry` is non-zero, only the symmetry-reduced portion of the domain is stored. `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity` track which chunks are in the reduced region.\n311\t\n312\t`ChunkSpec.face_halos[\"x.minus\"].electric_signs` and `.magnetic_signs` carry the pre-computed reflection signs per face from the `SymmetryAxisIR` data. These are the same signs used for PEC/PMC boundaries — the symmetry transform is a logical address transform applied during the halo exchange.\n313\t\n314\t### 7.2 Periodic and Bloch Remapping\n315\t\n316\tFor periodic axes, the `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects the `plus` face of the last chunk to the `minus` face of the first chunk (and vice versa). For Bloch, the exchange includes the `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n317\t\n318\t**Complex field storage:** `BoundarySpecIR.requires_complex_fields=True` when any Bloch axis is present. This flag forces complex dtype for all field arrays even when `bloch_vec == 0` for that axis.\n319\t\n320\t---\n321\t\n322\t## 8. Gaps and Deferred Design Points\n323\t\n324\tThe following are intentionally **deferred** beyond Phase 1:\n325\t\n326\t| Item | Reason for Deferral | Future Task |\n327\t|---|---|---|\n328\t| MPI halo exchange kernels | No MPI runner in Phase 1 | task-050+ |\n329\t| Dynamic load balancing | Static chunk layout sufficient for Phase 1 | Meep-style chunk_balancer |\n330\t| Chunk-local CFL computation | Global CFL sufficient for Phase 1; per-chunk min_step available in ChunkSpec for future | task-051 |\n331\t| Overlapped / systolic execution | Bulk-synchronous is the Phase 1 contract; ChunkLayout preserves the hooks | task-052+ |\n332\t| Non-uniform PML profile per chunk | PML coefficients are compiled globally; chunk-local profiles are a refinement | task-053 |\n333\t| Chunk-local subpixel materialization | Phase 1 subpixel uses AnisotropicMedium path; per-chunk averaging is a refinement | task-054 |\n334\t\n335\t---\n336\t\n337\t## 9. Handoff Points for Later Tasks\n338\t\n339\t### task-048 — Warp Timestepping Kernel\n340\t- Consume `ChunkLayout` and `ChunkSpec` to allocate field arrays per chunk\n341\t- Implement `electric_update_kernel` and `magnetic_update_kernel` with chunk-local index ranges\n342\t- Halo exchange becomes a separate kernel stage\n343\t\n344\t### task-049 — Runtime Executor\n345\t- Consume `CompiledSimulation` + `ChunkLayout` to build per-chunk field state\n346\t- Implement `run_chunked_simulation()` that loops over chunks, applies stage order, handles convergence\n347\t\n348\t### task-050 — MPI Halo Exchange\n349\t- Extend `ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` to multiple ranks\n350\t- Implement `mpi_halo_exchange()` using `exchange_plan`\n351\t- Collective operations for flux/mode monitor gathering\n352\t\n353\t### task-051 — CFL and Chunk Balancing\n354\t- Use `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape` and per-axis `cell_sizes` to compute per-chunk `min_step`\n355\t- If min_step varies across chunks, use the minimum for the global timestep\n356\t\n357\t### task-053 — Monitor Accumulation Kernels\n358\t- Use `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` to route recording to the owning chunk\n359\t- Implement gather/reduce for multi-chunk monitors (flux surfaces, mode overlaps)\n360\t\n361\t---\n362\t\n363\t## 10. chunk-aware exchange kernels that use `FaceHalo.exchange_kind` and `FaceHalo.phase_factor`.\n376\t\n377\t6. **`autofdtd/runtime/controls.py`** — extend `RuntimeController` to work with `ChunkLayout` and per-chunk step tracking.\n378\t\n379\t7. **`autofdtd/diagnostics/runtime_logging.py`** — extend `RuntimeExecutionLog` to include per-chunk throughput metrics (cells updated per second per Phase 1 Chunk Contract and Multi-Node Decomposition Architecture\n\n## Status\n\n**Draft — for review and task handoff**\n\n## 1. Background and Design Motivation\n\nPhase 1 (`task-047`) establishes the architectural contract for chunk-based decomposition before solver kernels are wired to a runtime executor. The goal is to define what a chunk owns, how chunks expose halo and monitor metadata, how chunk placement maps onto devices or nodes, and how this avoids blocking later systolic or overlapped execution phases.\n\n### Reference Foundations\n\n- **Meep chunk architecture** (`../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`): chunk is the stable decomposition unit, not MPI rank. A rank may own multiple chunks. Chunk boundaries align with PML layers and symmetry planes, not with MPI ranks. Communication plans are stage-specific and component-family-specific.\n- **Systolic FDTD scheduling** (`papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`): the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy. Per-face, per-stage halo semantics and interior-versus-boundary planning must be explicit now so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n- **Phase 1 codebase state**: `CompiledSimulation` in `compiler/pipeline.py` treats the entire grid as one monolithic entity. `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` is a global constant. No chunk index, rank, ownership mask, or per-chunk field decomposition exists yet.\n\n### Design Principles\n\n1. **Chunk is the unit of parallelism.** Rank or device is placement, not computation.\n2. **Logical and stored topology diverge.** Symmetry reduction and periodic/Bloch remapping are logical-address transforms over a full-domain view, not data-layout changes.\n3. **Scene compilation is separate from stepping.** Geometry and material initialization produce coefficient fields; those fields are then chunked for execution.\n4. **Bulk-synchronous by default, systolic-ready by design.** Phase 1 is bulk-synchronous, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics so overlap can be layered in later without rearchitecting the chunk contract.\n5. **Halo depth is per-face, not global.** Different boundary families (PML, ABC, Bloch, periodic) require different halo widths and different exchange semantics.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Chunk Data Model\n\n### 2.1 ChunkSpec — Planning Metadata\n\n`ChunkSpec` is a frozen planning metadata object produced during scene compilation (not a runtime data structure). It describes the intended decomposition before any field buffers are allocated.\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ChunkSpec:\n    \"\"\"Planning metadata for one chunk — produced during compilation.\"\"\"\n\n    chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int]          # logical position in chunk grid\n    global_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]            # full-domain bounds of this chunk (lo, hi)\n    interior_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]          # interior (non-halo) cell index range\n    owned_bounds: tuple[Vec3, Vec3]              # cells this chunk updates (including boundary)\n    local_grid_shape: tuple[int, int, int]       # full local array shape incl. ghost cells\n\n    # Per-face halo metadata\n    face_halos: dict[str, FaceHalo]              # key: \"x.minus\", \"x.plus\", \"y.minus\", ...\n\n    # Symmetry metadata\n    is_reduced: bool                             # True if chunk is in symmetry-reduced region\n    symmetry_multiplicity: int                   # how many full-domain points this chunk represents\n\n    # Ownership flags for sources and monitors\n    source_indices: tuple[int, ...]             # indices into compiled_sources owned here\n    monitor_indices: tuple[int, ...]              # indices into compiled_monitors owned here\n```\n\n**Key invariants:**\n- `interior_bounds` defines the region this chunk updates without reading halo data.\n- `owned_bounds` includes the one-cell-wide boundary ring that this chunk writes and other chunks read.\n- `local_grid_shape` is the shape of the full local array including ghost cells — this is what field buffers are allocated to.\n- For a chunk at chunk_index `(i, j, k)`, the global cell index range of its interior is `[interior_bounds.lo, interior_bounds.hi)` in grid coordinates.\n\n### 2.2 FaceHalo — Per-Face Halo Descriptor\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass FaceHalo:\n    \"\"\"Halo metadata for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n\n    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n    depth: int                                  # number of ghost cells on this face\n    neighbor_chunk_index: tuple[int, int, int] | None  # None = domain boundary\n    exchange_kind: Literal[\n        \"periodic\", \"bloch\", \"pec\", \"pmc\",\n        \"pml\", \"stable_pml\", \"absorber\", \"abc\",\n        \"symmetry_copy\", \"interior\"\n    ]\n    phase_factor: complex = 1.0 + 0.0j          # for Bloch: e^{i k · d}\n    electric_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection\n    magnetic_signs: tuple[int, int, int] = (1, 1, 1)   # for PEC/PMC/symmetry reflection\n    pml_profile: PMLFaceProfile | None = None   # for PML faces: coefficient profiles\n    abc_coefficients: ABCFaceCoefficients | None = None  # for ABC faces\n```\n\n**Exchange kinds:**\n- `periodic`: copy from neighbor's `plus` face to my `minus` ghost (or vice versa)\n- `bloch`: same as periodic but multiply by `phase_factor` (complex conjugate on minus side for H, no conjugate on E per Maxwell's convention)\n- `pec`: set ghost E to `-E_interior` reflected; H ghost from `+H_interior`\n- `pmc`: opposite parity of PEC\n- `pml` / `stable_pml` / `absorber`: staged damping update applied in PML stage (see Section 5)\n- `abc`: first-order Engquist-Majda absorption via one-step recurrence\n- `symmetry_copy`: fill ghost from interior using electric/magnetic sign transforms\n- `interior`: no exchange — this face is adjacent to another local chunk (same process/device)\n\n### 2.3 ChunkLayout — Global Decomposition Plan\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ChunkLayout:\n    \"\"\"Global chunk decomposition plan — immutable after compilation.\"\"\"\n\n    num_chunks: tuple[int, int, int]           # chunks per axis\n    total_chunks: int                          # product of above\n    chunks: tuple[ChunkSpec, ...]               # one per chunk\n    periodic_axes: tuple[str, ...]             # axes with periodic/Bloch boundary\n    bloch_axes: tuple[str, ...]                 # axes with Bloch boundary\n    exchange_plan: dict[str, ExchangeDescriptor]  # keyed by (\"chunk_a\", \"chunk_b\", \"face\")\n\n    # For device/rank mapping\n    device_assignment: tuple[int, ...]         # chunk_index → device_id (for CUDA)\n    rank_assignment: tuple[int, ...]            # chunk_index → MPI rank\n```\n\n**Key property:** `ChunkLayout.total_chunks` is the number of *logical* chunks, not necessarily the number of MPI ranks or CUDA devices. One rank/device may own multiple chunks.\n\n---\n\n## 3. What a Chunk Owns\n\nA chunk is the unit of **field storage and field update**. Each chunk owns:\n\n### 3.1 Field Arrays\n\nFor a chunk with `local_grid_shape = (Nx, Ny, Nz)`:\n\n| Array | Shape | Staggering |\n|---|---|---|\n| `electric_field` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` | Yee electric cell centers |\n| `magnetic_field` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` | Yee magnetic cell centers |\n| `eps_xx`, `eps_yy`, `eps_zz` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | permittivity on E-grid |\n| `mu_xx`, `mu_yy`, `mu_zz` | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | permeability on H-grid |\n| `sigma_e` (optional) | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | electric conductivity |\n| `sigma_h` (optional) | `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` | magnetic conductivity |\n\n**Note:** These are *local* arrays including ghost cells. The interior region `[interior_bounds.lo, interior_bounds.hi)` contains valid owned field values. Ghost cells are filled by the boundary exchange stage before each update.\n\n### 3.2 Dispersive Auxiliary state\n\nFor PoleResidue media, per pole per component:\n\n```\npol_x[p, i, j, k], pol_y[p, i, j, k], pol_z[p, i, j, k]  # polarization currents\n```\n\nShape: `(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz)` per component axis. These are allocated only for chunks that contain dispersive material.\n\n### 3.3 Anisotropic Auxiliary State\n\nFor diagonal AnisotropicMedia, the six `eps_xx` etc. arrays above are sufficient (no extra auxiliary state). If Phase 2 introduces fully anisotropic media, additional off-diagonal arrays would be needed.\n\n### 3.4 PML/ABC Boundary state\n\nPer face with PML/ABC:\n\n```\nPML memory: dict[face_key, np.ndarray]   # shape from PMLFaceState.memory_shape\nABC previous: dict[face_key, np.ndarray]  # shape from ABCFaceState.value_shape\n```\n\nThese are allocated per chunk per absorbing face, not global.\n\n### 3.5 Source State\n\nSources are not stored per chunk persistently. Instead, each chunk's `ChunkSpec.source_indices` tells which sources from `CompiledSources` inject into this chunk's domain. At runtime, source injection kernels use the compiled source metadata (placements, amplitude weights) to add source contributions directly into the chunk's `electric_field` or `magnetic_field` arrays.\n\n### 3.6 Monitor State\n\nSimilarly, monitor recording state is per-chunk. For field monitors:\n- `FieldMonitorState` per monitor that overlaps this chunk\n- DFT accumulation buffers for frequency-domain monitors\n\nFor flux monitors:\n- `FluxMonitorState` per monitor with surfaces intersecting this chunk\n\nChunk-local monitor data is gathered by a monitor collection stage after each recording interval.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Halo and Monitor Metadata\n\n### 4.1 Halo Exchange Protocol\n\nHalo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**, following the pattern established in the boundary compiler but scoped per chunk pair:\n\n```\nTimestep stage order per chunk:\n  1. [boundary_exchange]    — fill ghost cells from neighbors\n  2. [electric_update]       — update E in interior + owned-boundary cells\n  3. [source_injection]      — add source contributions to E\n  4. [boundary_exchange]     — re-exchange E ghosts (needed for H update)\n  5. [magnetic_update]        — update H in interior + owned-boundary cells\n  6. [monitor_collection]     — record E/H at monitor points (interval filter)\n  7. [pml_stage]             — apply PML/ABC damping to E and H ghosts\n  8. [convergence_check]     — (on interval) compute integrated E², check shutoff\n```\n\n**Key property:** The `boundary_exchange` stage operates on ghost cells only. Interior cells are never communicated during the timestep — only at the start of the step for halo fill and after the step for PML memory update.\n\n### 4.2 Halo Depth\n\nHalo depth is **per-face**, not a global constant. Typical depths:\n\n| Boundary Type | Halo Depth | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| Periodic / Bloch | 1 cell | standard Yee ghost cell |\n| PEC / PMC | 1 cell | ghost set by reflection transform |\n| PML | variable (num_layers) | full PML depth, staggered |\n| ABC (first-order) | 1 cell | single-step recurrence |\n| Symmetry | 1 cell | copy from interior with sign transform |\n| Interior (chunk neighbor) | 1 cell | local memory copy |\n\n**The current `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced** with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` from the `ChunkLayout`.\n\n### 4.3 Monitor Overlap with Chunks\n\nA monitor may span multiple chunks. Monitor metadata in `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` tells which monitors overlap this chunk. Monitor data is accumulated per-chunk and gathered by a global collection pass.\n\nFor a `FieldMonitor` with interval/start semantics:\n- Each chunk computes which of its interior cells fall within the monitor's spatial region\n- Recording is done by the chunk that owns the cell\n- Gathered data is assembled in rank-order for `SimulationData`\n\nFor a `FluxMonitor`:\n- The flux surface may be split across multiple chunks\n- Each chunk computes its portion of the Poynting flux integral\n- Partial sums are reduced across chunks\n\n---\n\n## 5. PML / ABC State Decomposition\n\nPML and ABC state must be decomposed per-chunk per-face. The current monolithic `PMLBoundaryState` in the codebase must be replaced.\n\n### 5.1 PML Face State (per chunk, per face)\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ChunkPMLFaceState:\n    \"\"\"PML state for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n\n    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n    memory_shape: tuple[int, ...]         # transverse shape of the PML layer\n    sigma_profile: np.ndarray               # compiled sigma (sigma_min..sigma_max polynomial)\n    kappa_profile: np.ndarray               # compiled kappa\n    alpha_profile: np.ndarray               # compiled alpha\n    memory: np.ndarray                      # persistent PML memory (attenuation term)\n```\n\n### 5.2 ABC Face State (per chunk, per face)\n\n```python\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ChunkABCFaceState:\n    \"\"\"First-order ABC state for one face of one chunk.\"\"\"\n\n    axis: Literal[\"x\", \"y\", \"z\"]\n    side: Literal[\"minus\", \"plus\"]\n    value_shape: tuple[int, ...]            # transverse shape of the ABC surface\n    previous_boundary: np.ndarray           # E or H at previous timestep on boundary\n    previous_adjacent: np.ndarray           # E or H at previous timestep one cell inside\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. Device and Node Placement\n\n### 6.1 CUDA GPU Placement\n\nThe `ChunkLayout.device_assignment` field maps each logical `chunk_index` to a CUDA device ID. For Phase 1 single-GPU or dual-GPU smoke tests:\n\n- All chunks map to device 0 (single-GPU fallback)\n- Or chunks 0..N//2 on device 0, chunks N//2..N on device 1\n\n**This mapping is a planning artifact, not the execution runtime.** The actual field arrays are allocated on the target device via CUDA managed memory or explicit device arrays.\n\n### 6.2 Multi-Node / MPI Placement\n\n`ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` maps each logical chunk to an MPI rank. For Phase 1, this is always all chunks to rank 0 (no MPI decomposition). Later phases extend to per-rank ownership with MPI halo exchange.\n\n**Separation of concerns:**\n- `ChunkSpec` is pure planning metadata — no field data, no communication\n- `ChunkLayout` adds device/rank placement hints\n- Actual field arrays and PML state are allocated at runtime using `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape`\n- Halo exchange uses `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` to know which chunk pairs need to communicate\n\n### 6.3 Warp Kernel Conventions\n\nEach chunk's field arrays are Warp-compatible `wp.array` objects allocated on the target device. Warp kernel launches use chunk-local index ranges:\n\n```python\n# Pseudo-code for chunk-local kernel launch\nchunk = chunks[chunk_id]\nwp.launch(\n    electric_update_kernel,\n    dim=chunk.local_grid_shape,\n    inputs=[chunk.electric_field, chunk.magnetic_field, chunk.eps, chunk.mu],\n    device=chunk.device_id,\n)\n```\n\nHalo exchange is implemented as separate Warp kernels that read from neighbor chunks' field arrays and write to ghost cells.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Symmetry and Logical vs. Stored Topology\n\n### 7.1 Symmetry Reduction\n\nWhen `Simulation.symmetry` is non-zero, only the symmetry-reduced portion of the domain is stored. `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity` track which chunks are in the reduced region.\n\n`ChunkSpec.face_halos[\"x.minus\"].electric_signs` and `.magnetic_signs` carry the pre-computed reflection signs per face from the `SymmetryAxisIR` data. These are the same signs used for PEC/PMC boundaries — the symmetry transform is a logical address transform applied during the halo exchange.\n\n### 7.2 Periodic and Bloch Remapping\n\nFor periodic axes, the `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects the `plus` face of the last chunk to the `minus` face of the first chunk (and vice versa). For Bloch, the exchange includes the `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n\n**Complex field storage:** `BoundarySpecIR.requires_complex_fields=True` when any Bloch axis is present. This flag forces complex dtype for all field arrays even when `bloch_vec == 0` for that axis.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Gaps and Deferred Design Points\n\nThe following are intentionally **deferred** beyond Phase 1:\n\n| Item | Reason for Deferral | Future Task |\n|---|---|---|\n| MPI halo exchange kernels | No MPI runner in Phase 1 | task-050+ |\n| Dynamic load balancing | Static chunk layout sufficient for Phase 1 | Meep-style chunk_balancer |\n| Chunk-local CFL computation | Global CFL sufficient for Phase 1; per-chunk min_step available in ChunkSpec for future | task-051 |\n| Overlapped / systolic execution | Bulk-synchronous is the Phase 1 contract; ChunkLayout preserves the hooks | task-052+ |\n| Non-uniform PML profile per chunk | PML coefficients are compiled globally; chunk-local profiles are a refinement | task-053 |\n| Chunk-local subpixel materialization | Phase 1 subpixel uses AnisotropicMedium path; per-chunk averaging is a refinement | task-054 |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Handoff Points for Later Tasks\n\n### task-048 — Warp Timestepping Kernel\n- Consume `ChunkLayout` and `ChunkSpec` to allocate field arrays per chunk\n- Implement `electric_update_kernel` and `magnetic_update_kernel` with chunk-local index ranges\n- Halo exchange becomes a separate kernel stage\n\n### task-049 — Runtime Executor\n- Consume `CompiledSimulation` + `ChunkLayout` to build per-chunk field state\n- Implement `run_chunked_simulation()` that loops over chunks, applies stage order, handles convergence\n\n### task-050 — MPI Halo Exchange\n- Extend `ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` to multiple ranks\n- Implement `mpi_halo_exchange()` using `exchange_plan`\n- Collective operations for flux/mode monitor gathering\n\n### task-051 — CFL and Chunk Balancing\n- Use `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape` and per-axis `cell_sizes` to compute per-chunk `min_step`\n- If min_step varies across chunks, use the minimum for the global timestep\n\n### task-053 — Monitor Accumulation Kernels\n- Use `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` to route recording to the owning chunk\n- Implement gather/reduce for multi-chunk monitors (flux surfaces, mode overlaps)\n\n---\n\n## 10. Summary of Required Changes\n\nTo realize this contract in code, the following changes are needed:\n\n1. **New module `autofdtd/runtime/chunk.py`** with `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, `ChunkLayout`, `ChunkPMLFaceState`, `ChunkABCFaceState`.\n\n2. **`autofdtd/compiler/pipeline.py`** extended to produce `ChunkLayout` after `CompiledSimulation`.\n\n3. **`autofdtd/compiler/boundaries.py`** — replace `halo_depth: int = 1` with per-face `FaceHalo` descriptors attached to each `ChunkSpec`.\n\n4. **`autofdtd/ir/models.py`** — add `ChunkSpecIR`, `FaceHaloIR`, `ChunkLayoutIR` as versioned IR transport types for chunk decomposition.\n\n5. **`autofdtd/kernels/boundaries.py`** — refactor `_ghost_and_source_slices` into chunk-aware exchange kernels that use `FaceHalo.exchange_kind` and `FaceHalo.phase_factor`.\n\n6. **`autofdtd/runtime/controls.py`** — extend `RuntimeController` to work with `ChunkLayout` and per-chunk step tracking.\n\n7. **`autofdtd/diagnostics/runtime_logging.py`** — extend `RuntimeExecutionLog` to include per-chunk throughput metrics (cells updated per second per chunk).\n\n---\n\n## References\n\n- Meep: `../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`\n- Meep Parallel: `../meep/doc/docs/Parallel_Meep.md`\n- Systolic FDTD: `papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`\n- GPU Benchmarking: `papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf`\n- Meep paper: `papers/meep_paper.pdf`\n- Current `CompiledSimulation`: `src/autofdtd/compiler/pipeline.py`\n- Current `CompiledBoundarySpec`: `src/autofdtd/compiler/boundaries.py`\n- Current `RuntimeController`: `src/autofdtd/runtime/controls.py`\n","numLines":393,"startLine":1,"totalLines":393}}}

The cell at `nx-1` is a boundary cell available for derivative computations, but I'm not actually updating it—I'm only computing derivatives at interior points `1` through `nx-2`.\n\nLet me trace through the bounds check more carefully. The condition `i >= nx-1` means `i` ranges from `1` to `nx-2` inclusive, which are exactly the interior cells I want to update. For a 1D grid with indices `0, 1, 2, ..., nx-1`, the interior points are indeed `1` through `nx-2`. So the bounds check is correct—I'm only updating interior cells where all array accesses remain valid.\n\nLet me reconsider the dimensional requirements. For a proper 3D Yee lattice with PML boundaries, I need nx, ny, nz grid points where the interior cells exclude the boundary layers. This means nx, ny, nz should be at least 4 to have meaningful interior updates. Let me verify with a concrete example: if nx=5, then indices 0 through 4 exist, with interior cells at 1, 2, 3. This satisfies the condition i < nx-1 since 3 < 4 is true.\n\nNow I need to fix the actual implementation. The existing code incorrectly uses flat indexing for a 3D array structure—it treats the array as one-dimensional when it's actually (nx, ny, nz, 3). For a 3D Yee lattice, each thread should correspond to a cell (i, j, k) and compute the curl of H or E to update the field components.\n\nThe corrected approach uses proper 3D indexing with component offsets. I need to verify that accessing neighboring cells like `j+1` won't exceed bounds—the interior cell check `i < nx-1` ensures we don't go out of bounds when accessing `i+1` since the last valid interior cell is `nx-2` and `nx-1` is the boundary.\n\nHowever, `Hy[i, j+1, k, 1]` is problematic when `j = ny-2` because `ny-1` is a boundary cell that may not be properly initialized in vacuum. The issue is that when computing the curl at interior points, I'm reading from boundary cells which could contain garbage values in a non-PML setup.\n\nThe correct approach requires ghost cells to be filled before each update. In the standard FDTD scheme with PML boundaries, the domain actually spans indices `0` to `nx-1` where `0` and `nx-1` are the PML layers, and interior cells are `1` through `nx-2`. This ensures all curl computations have valid data to read from.\n\nFor a vacuum propagation case without PML, ghost cells get filled with boundary condition values—periodic copies, PEC/PMC reflections, or first-order ABC values. The `boundary_exchange` stage handles this. Since the task description specifies vacuum propagation, I'm assuming proper ghost cell initialization occurs before the update, so the interior cell access pattern `1` to `nx-2` should work correctly.\n\nLooking at the original kernel implementation, it uses flat indexing which is incorrect for 3D arrays. The proper approach requires multi-dimensional indexing for the Yee lattice scheme.\n\nNow I'll implement the corrected kernel following the same pattern: bounds checking on interior cells, then computing the curl terms for each field component using the Yee lattice spacing.","signature":"911c27eb362c9fd9827976dbd48e0c6fa7ccb78496257893f8d703467c336ee6"}],"model":"MiniMax-M2.7","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":42430,"output_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":0,"cache_read_input_tokens":23360},"service_tier":"standard","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b12143ec-27d0-4fe6-a81a-c82ccae4f4e4","uuid":"0730f879-ab58-4c86-8840-3daa85996056"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_function_j1m2sd7favl6_1","type":"tool_result","content":"\"\"\"Core Maxwell timestepping kernels for Phase 1.\n\nThis module provides the GPU-accelerated E and H field update kernels.\nThe kernels follow the Warp backend conventions documented in ``backend.py``.\n\nKernel Organization\n------------------\nThe Maxwell update is split into explicit stages:\n\n1. **boundary_exchange** — fill ghost cells before each update\n2. **electric_update** — update electric field components\n3. **source_injection** — add source contributions to E\n4. **boundary_exchange** — re-exchange E ghosts before H update\n5. **magnetic_update** — update magnetic field components\n6. **pml_stage** — apply PML/ABC damping (post-H update)\n7. **convergence_check** — integrated field intensity (Python-side)\n\nUpdate Equations\n---------------\nFor a Yee lattice with cell sizes ``(dx, dy, dz)`` and timestep ``dt``:\n\nElectric field update::\n\n    Ex[i+1] = Ex[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hz[i,j+1,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Hy[i,j,k])/dx - (Hx[i,j+1,k] - Hx[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nMagnetic field update::\n\n    Hx[i+1] = Hx[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ez[i,j+1,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dy - (Ey[i,j,k+1] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nCoefficients\n-----------\nFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n\n    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n\nIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\nthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n\n- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n- ``time``: float current simulation time\n\nSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\narguments. The actual implementations follow\n# the Yee lattice update equations documented in this module's docstring.\n\nif WARP_AVAILABLE:\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def electric_update_3d(\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        eps_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        eps_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        eps_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update electric field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Ex, Ey, Ez at interior cells using the\n        curl of the magnetic field. skip ghost cells\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j < 1 or j >= ny - 1 or k < 1 or k >= nz - 1:\n            return\n\n        idx = i + j * nx + k * nx * ny\n\n        # Ex[i,j,k] update using curl(H) at (i, j+1/2, k+1/2)\n        # dHz/dy - dHy/dz\n        Hz_im1 = H[(i + 1) + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 2 * nx * ny * nz]\n        Hz_i = H[i + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 2 * nx * ny * nz]\n        Hy_im1 = H[(i + 1) + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 1 * nx * ny * nz]\n        Hy_i = H[i + j * nx + k * nx * ny + 1 * nx * ny * nz]\n\n        # Actually we need neighboring cells for the curl\n        # Using simple central difference approximation\n        # Ex update: (Hz[j+1] - Hz[j])/dy - (Hy[k+1] - Hy[k])/dz\n        # For simplicity, use first-order neighbor\n        dHy_dz = (Hy_i - Hy_im1) / dz if k > 0 else 0.0\n        dHz_dy = (Hz_i - Hz_im1) / dy if j > 0 else 0.0\n\n        eps = eps_xx[idx]\n        ce = dt / (eps + 1e-20)  # avoid division by zero\n\n        E[idx] += ce * (dHz_dy - dHy_dz)\n\n    @wp.kernel\n    def magnetic_update_3d(\n        H: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        E: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        mu_xx: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        mu_yy: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        mu_zz: wp.array(dtype=wp.float32),\n        dt: float,\n        dx: float,\n        dy: float,\n        dz: float,\n        nx: int,\n        ny: int,\n        nz: int,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Update magnetic field components for 3D Yee lattice.\n\n        This kernel updates Hx, Hy, Hz at interior cells using the\n        curl of the electric field.           dx, dy, dz: Cell sizes\n            nx, ny, nz: Grid dimensions\n        \"\"\"\n        i, j, k = wp.tid()\n\n        # Bounds check - skip ghost cells\n        if i < 1 or i >= nx - 1 or j approximation as electric update\n        mu = mu_xx[idx]\n        ch = -dt / (mu + 1e-20)  # note the negative sign for H update\n\n        H[idx] += ch * 0.0  # Placeholder - full curl computation needed\n\nelse:\n    # Stub definitions when Warp is unavailable\n    electric_update_3d = None\n    magnetic_update_3d = None\n\n\n# get_warp_device(device_id: int | None = None) -> \"wp.Device | None\":\n    \"\"\"Get a Warp device for allocation.\"\"\"\n    if not WARP_AVAILABLE:\n        return None\n    if device_id is None:\n        return wp.get_device()\n    return wp.get_device(device_id)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Stepping helper\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef step_maxwell(\n    arrays: dict[str, \"wp.array | np.ndarray\"],\n    dt: float,\n    dx: float,\n    dy: float,\n    dz: float,\n    *,\n    step_index: int = 0,\n    time: float = 0.0,\n    device: int | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Execute one Maxwell timestep using the allocated arrays.\n\n Maxwell timestepping kernels for Phase 1.\n\nThis module provides the GPU-accelerated E and H field update kernels.\nThe kernels follow the Warp backend conventions documented in ``backend.py``.\n\nKernel Organization\n------------------\nThe Maxwell update is split into explicit stages:\n\n1. **boundary_exchange** — fill ghost cells before each update\n2. **electric_update** — update electric field components\n3. **source_injection** — add source contributions to E\n4. **boundary_exchange** — re-exchange E ghosts before H update\n5. **magnetic_update** — update magnetic field components\n6. **pml_stage** — apply PML/ABC damping (post-H update)\n7. **convergence_check** — integrated field intensity (Python-side)\n\nUpdate Equations\n---------------\nFor a Yee lattice with cell sizes ``(dx, dy, dz)`` and timestep ``dt``:\n\nElectric field update::\n\n    Ex[i+1] = Ex[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hz[i,j+1,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hy[i+1,j,k] -   # for H update (note the sign)\n    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n\nIn a homogeneous medium
